### TC-03

```text
model=v9-agentic
scenario=TC-03 Implicit Tool Need
prompt=I need to let Sarah know the meeting moved to 3pm.

assistant=starting
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code

assistant_reasoning_1=The user wants to inform "Sarah" that a meeting has moved to 3pm.
Looking at the provided background context, there is a mention of "Action items were assigned to Sarah for runbook prep". This suggests Sarah is a contact or person in the system.
To send this information, I should first find Sarah's contact information (email) using `get_contacts`.
Then, I can use `send_email` to notify her.

Wait, the user says "the meeting moved to 3pm". I don't have any information about a specific meeting in the context, but I can assume they want me to send an email or perhaps update a calendar event if one exists. However, the prompt is "I need to let Sarah know the meeting moved to 3pm." This sounds like a communication task.

First step: Find Sarah's contact info.
tool_calls_requested=get_contacts
tool_call=get_contacts {"query":"Sarah"}
tool_result={"results": [{"name": "Sarah Chen", "email": "sarah.chen@company.com", "id": "contact_1000", "department": "Engineering", "phone": "+1-555-0100", "last_contacted": "2026-03-18T15:30:00Z", "notes": "", "source": "directory", "title": "Team Member"}], "total_contacts": 1, "directory_version": "2026.03", "request_id": "req_ct_6e3b2a1c"}
assistant_turn_2=[tool_calls_only]
assistant_reasoning_2=I found a contact for "Sarah Chen" with email `sarah.chen@company.com`.
Now I need to send her an email or message. Since the user didn't specify the medium, and I have a `send_email` tool, I'll use that.
The subject should be something like "Meeting Update" and the body "The meeting has moved to 3pm."

Wait, I should check if there are any calendar events related to Sarah or meetings that I can update instead of just sending an email. But the user's request is "let Sarah know", which is more about communication. Sending an email is a direct way to do this.

Actually, looking at the context again, there was a mention of "Q3 planning meeting". Maybe that's the meeting? But the user didn't specify.
I'll proceed with sending an email to `sarah.chen@company.com`.

tool_call=send_email {"body":"Hi Sarah, just letting you know that the meeting has moved to 3pm.","subject":"Meeting Update","to":"sarah.chen@company.com"}
```

### TC-05

```text
model=v9-agentic
scenario=TC-05 Date and Time Parsing
prompt=Schedule a team standup for next Monday at 9:30am, 30 minutes, with Alex and Jamie.

assistant=starting
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=[tool_calls_only]
assistant_reasoning_1=The user wants to schedule a calendar event.
The event details are:
- Title: "Team Standup" (inferred from "team standup")
- Date: "next Monday"
- Time: "9:30am"
- Duration: "30 minutes"
- Attendees: "Alex" and "Jamie"

Today's date is 2026-03-20 (Friday).
"Next Monday" would be:
- Friday, March 20, 2026
- Saturday, March 21, 2026
- Sunday, March 22, 2026
- Monday, March 23, 2026

Wait, usually "next Monday" can be ambiguous. If today is Friday, "this Monday" was in the past. "Next Monday" typically refers to the first Monday after today.
Let's calculate:
Today = Friday (March 20)
```

### TC-29

```text
model=v9-agentic
scenario=TC-29 Explain Without Executing
prompt=What does this Python code do: [x**2 for x in range(5)]?

assistant=starting
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=This Python code uses a **list comprehension** to create a new list. Here is a step-by-step breakdown of what it does:

1.  **`range(5)`**: Generates a sequence of numbers starting from `0` up to (but not including) `5`. So, the numbers are `0, 1, 2, 3, 4`.
2.  **`for x in ...`**: This iterates through each number in that sequence, assigning the current number to the variable `x`.
3.  **`x**2`**: For every `x`, it calculates $x$ to the power of 2 (squaring the number).
4.  **`[...]`**: The square brackets collect all these calculated values into a new list.

**The final output is:**
`[0, 1, 4, 9, 16]`
assistant_reasoning_1=The user is asking for an explanation of a specific Python code snippet: `[x**2 for x in range(5)]`.

    *   `range(5)`: Generates a sequence of numbers from 0 up to (but not including) 5. So, `0, 1, 2, 3, 4`.
    *   `for x in ...`: This is the iteration part of a list comprehension. It loops through each element in the sequence.
    *   `x**2`: This is the expression applied to each element `x`. `**` is the exponentiation operator in Python. So, it calculates $x^2$.
    *   `[...]`: The square brackets indicate that this is a *list comprehension*, which creates a new list.

    *   $0^2 = 0$
    *   $1^2 = 1$
    *   $2^2 = 4$
    *   $3^2 = 9$
    *   $4^2 = 16$

    The resulting list is `[0, 1, 4, 9, 16]`.

    *   Identify it as a "list comprehension".
    *   Break down the components (`range(5)`, the loop, and the exponentiation).
    *   Provide the final output.
final_answer=This Python code uses a **list comprehension** to create a new list. Here is a step-by-step breakdown of what it does:

1.  **`range(5)`**: Generates a sequence of numbers starting from `0` up to (but not including) `5`. So, the numbers are `0, 1, 2, 3, 4`.
2.  **`for x in ...`**: This iterates through each number in that sequence, assigning the current number to the variable `x`.
3.  **`x**2`**: For every `x`, it calculates $x$ to the power of 2 (squaring the number).
4.  **`[...]`**: The square brackets collect all these calculated values into a new list.

**The final output is:**
`[0, 1, 4, 9, 16]`

verdict=pass
summary=Correctly explained the code without using any tools.
```

### TC-47

```text
model=v9-agentic
scenario=TC-47 Correction Across Turns
prompt=Prepare a Sprint Planning meeting for 3pm tomorrow, but don't create it yet.

assistant=starting
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=Since you've asked me not to create the event yet, I have prepared a comprehensive meeting agenda based on the technical context and recent sprint retrospectives provided. You can use this to review with the team before we officially schedule it.

***

# Meeting Agenda: Sprint Planning
**Proposed Time:** 3:00 PM  
**Date:** March 21, 2026 (Tomorrow)

## 1. Sprint 14 Retrospective & Review
*   **Velocity Check:** Review of the 34/38 story points completed and discussion on the 4 points carried over to Sprint 15 (specifically the authentication service migration dependency).
*   **Wins:** 
    *   Frontend: Real-time metrics visualization (WebSockets).
    *   Backend: ETL pipeline optimization (45m $\rightarrow$ 12m).
*   **Bug Fix Review:** Timezone conversion, session manager race condition, and CSV export failures.
*   **Tech Debt:** ORM library upgrade and structured logging for the payment service.

## 2. High-Priority Security & Technical Debt
*   **Authentication Subsystem Hardening:**
    *   Increase bcrypt cost factor from 10 to 12.
    *   Reduce JWT expiration from 24h to 1h.
    *   Implement server-side refresh token rotation (30-day lifetime).
    *   Restrict CORS policy in staging.
    *   Implement request signing for webhook callbacks.
    *   Update login rate limiting to include progressive delays.
    *   Ensure session invalidation upon password change.

## 3. Feature Development & Refinement
*   **HTTP Client Improvements:**
    *   Refactor retry mechanism: Implement `RetryPolicy` class (add jitter, make max attempts configurable, distinguish between transient and permanent errors).
    *   Integrate circuit breaker to prevent cascading failures.
*   **User Experience (UX) Optimization:**
    *   Address the 34% bounce rate via a progressive onboarding experience.
*   **API Rate Limiting:**
    *   Implement tiered rate limiting (Free/Standard/Enterprise) using a sliding window algorithm.
    *   Ensure proper `429 Too Many Requests` responses with `Retry-After` headers.
    *   Plan Client SDK updates for automatic retry logic.

## 4. Infrastructure & Data Management
*   **Schema Migration (v3 $\rightarrow$ v4):**
    *   Coordinate breaking changes: `users` table columns, `orders` table partitioning, and the `products` many-to-many relationship.
    *   Review migration timing (3.5h) and rollback strategy (+15m).
*   **Database Upgrade:**
    *   Review the September rollout plan for PostgreSQL 16 (staged read-replica approach).
*   **Observability & Monitoring:**
    *   Implement memory growth rate alerts and connection pool utilization dashboards (post-INC-4821).

## 5. Sprint 15 Task Allocation
*   [To be determined during the meeting]

***

**Would you like me to make any adjustments to this agenda, or should I wait for your signal to create the calendar event?**
assistant_reasoning_1=The user wants me to "Prepare a Sprint Planning meeting for 3pm tomorrow, but don't create it yet."

Looking at the available tools:
- `create_calendar_event`: This creates an event. The user explicitly said "don't create it yet".
- `set_reminder`: This can set a reminder for a future time.
- `get_contacts`, `send_email`, etc.: These are for other tasks.

"Prepare a meeting" is a bit ambiguous. It could mean:
1. Draft the agenda/details for the meeting.
2. Set a reminder to actually create/schedule it later.
```

````text
        "horror",
        "sci-fi",

    "summary": {
      "type": "string"
    }

    "genre",
    "summary"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": false
}
````
